### §4 Decisions and questions to Taylor

#### §4.1 Montana items

**Item 8 (shared rows)**

- smoking-policy: still not tagged MT (42 states; Montana answers with smoking-policy-mt, MT log s6.3.2). Confirmed 2026-11-14.
- no-alterations: tagged MT; fits as written. Its last sentence saves any modification 'applicable law entitles Tenant to perform', which covers Mont. Code Ann. § 49-2-305(5)(a)(i) (reasonable modifications at the disabled tenant's expense; landlord may, when reasonable, condition permission on restoring the interior) and FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. § 100.203(a)-(b) (same restoration condition; landlord may not increase the customary deposit, but may negotiate an interest-bearing restoration escrow; may require a description, workmanlike assurances and permits). The 2026-10-04 central edit (ownership of an installation the law makes the tenant's) only defers to law; Montana has no statute making a tenant installation the tenant's property (see the telecom-access, portable-solar, portable-cooling-device and ev-charging-* rows for the batteries). Confirmed.
- addendum-precedence: tagged MT; fits. Montana's required writings (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-301(1) disclosure; Mont. Code Ann. § 70-25-206(1) condition statement; Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-311(2) written rules; the federal lead disclosure) still control under the 'applicable law requires' exception; the 2026-10-04 central edit only adds documents that law says control. Confirmed.
- default-by-tenant: fits. Its fee sentence ('To the extent permitted under applicable law, the prevailing party may recover') matches Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-442(1) ('notwithstanding an agreement to the contrary'); its 'reasonable costs and expenses' are not an indemnity for the landlord's own purposeful misconduct or negligence (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-202(3)). No override needed.
- keys (rekey charge): fits. A rekey cost on the tenant's failure to return keys is 'other money owing to the landlord at the time of deduction' and may come out of the deposit (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-25-201(1)); no indemnity for the landlord's own fault (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-202(3)); no attorney-fee term (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-442(1)). No override needed.
- hoa-compliance (fine reimbursement): fits. An association fine caused by the tenant's violation is a lease charge, deductible as 'penalties due under lease provisions, and other money owing' (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-25-201(1)); it is not an indemnity for the landlord's fault. Soft point, not an override: association rules the lease makes binding operate like landlord rules, which are enforceable only if the tenant had notice and they are given in writing (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-311(1)(f), (2)); see finding F-6.

**Item 9 (early termination, casualty, CARES)**

- (a) early-termination-mt fits Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-201(2)(f) (fee capped at one month's rent; rent to a new tenancy or term end under Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-426(3)); 2025 Mont. Laws ch. 768 (HB 810) amended Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-201 but its changes are the payment-method provisions ((2)(b), (4)); (2)(f) reads as the row quotes. Its saving sentence preserves 'a servicemember's right to end this Lease after being ordered to a new permanent duty station without paying an early termination fee or other damages' as an example ('including'), so it does not narrow FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955, which also covers entry into service, deployment of 90 days or more, stop-movement orders, death and catastrophic injury, bars 'an early termination charge' ((e)(1)), and sets the effective date ((d)(1)(A)). Fits. See finding F-3 on edu-servicemember-mt.
- (b) Fault persons differ. casualty-termination-mt uses Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-409(2)'s exact words ('purposeful or negligent act of Tenant or Tenant's family or guests'). tenant-caused-damage-mt uses 'purposeful or negligent act or omission of Tenant, a member of Tenant's family, or a guest or other person on the premises with Tenant's consent' for damage from 'fire, water, a vehicle or any other cause'. That set matches Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-406(1)(a)(iii) and Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-408(3), but for FIRE OR CASUALTY it is wider than Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-409(2), which reaches only an 'act' of 'the tenant or the tenant's family or guests', and 'Guest' means 'a person staying with a tenant for a temporary period of time' (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-103(8)). See finding F-2.
- (c) No MT eviction row says federal law adds nothing, so none contradicts edu-cares-act-notice (tagged MT). Soft edge, same as ND s6.10: edu-nonpayment-notice-mt says that after 3 days 'the tenant must vacate', and edu-noncompliance-cure-mt and edu-dangerous-activity-eviction-mt give 3-, 5- and 14-day move-out dates, all without the federal overlay; for a covered property FEDERAL: 15 U.S.C. § 9058(c)(1) bars requiring the tenant to vacate before 30 days after a notice to vacate. edu-eviction-process-mt's notes say 'Federal CARES Act notice not read (MT log §1.4)'; it has now been read whole (FEDERAL: 15 U.S.C. § 9058, uscode.house.gov, 2026-11-14). edu-for-cause-eviction-mt, edu-end-of-term-mt, default-by-tenant's MT segment and criminal-activity-mt state no move-out period that conflicts. See finding F-4.

**Item 11 (open items)**

- Ch. 656: the codified Mont. Code Ann. § 70-25-202(2) reads 'and the landlord has a pending claim filed in court' (History: amd. Sec. 2, Ch. 656, L. 2025); Claude Code confirmed from the PNGs (checks/ch656-strike-p1.png, -p3.png) that 'for actual damages' is struck. MT log s7.2 is closed: the compiled text is the law. security-deposit-return-mt still says it (finding F-1); MT log s6.3.7's narrower-reading decision is superseded. Mont. Code Ann. § 70-25-201(3)(b) (also amended by ch. 656, § 1) uses the same 'pending claim filed in court' words.
- Section 70-24-303 changeover (temporary version to January 1, 2031; 'Subject to 27-1-1603', the covid-19 premises-liability limit) and the portal rule (Rules for Access to the Trial Court Public Record Portal, Section 4.30) are legal-watch items, not rows.

- **Item 1 (corpus channel and currency):** see §1; every cited section's History line read and every 2025 act it names read; versions stated where a row relies on a two-version section; federal law read in the built-in browser (the shell and WebFetch could not reach uscode.house.gov or ecfr.gov); no bot block or CAPTCHA was met.
- **Item 4 (domestic violence):** six dv-* rows (load L03), each pointing to edu-no-dv-termination-mt; Title 40, ch. 15 read (protection-order law has not moved; no 2025 act amended a ch. 15 section); whole-corpus batteries on Montana's own terms in both word orders; FEDERAL: 34 U.S.C. § 12491 stated only for covered federal housing programs.
- **Item 5 (deposits, fees, rent):** loads L03 and L04; tags tenant-caused-fines-oh, detector-care-la, rent-installments-or, rent-concession-wi; Montana rows fees-and-charges-mt, government-fee-reimbursement-mt, carpet-cleaning-mt; no deposit row says 'for actual damages'.
- **Item 6 (local limits, inspection, preemption):** load L05; every row says whether a state statute or only local code reaches the topic and whether Mont. Code Ann. § 7-1-111 preempts (self-government units only; Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-102(2)(c) makes the act exclusive statewide); the Land Use Planning Act exemption (Mont. Code Ann. § 76-25-105(4)) is stated where it matters.
- **Item 7 (utilities, building safety, other):** loads L07, L08, L09; PSC rules and Title 69 read; building and fire codes read to the corpus boundary (model codes not in the corpus, stated in each row); detector placement rules read (finding F-5).
- **Item 12 (general statutes):** load L11: unregistered foreign entities (Mont. Code Ann. §§ 35-8-1001, 35-8-1002, 35-14-1502), assumed names (Mont. Code Ann. § 30-13-215), service on a nonresident owner (Montana Rules of Civil Procedure, Rule 4, court rule), entity representation in justice court (Justice and City Court Rules of Civil Procedure, Rule 2(a), court rule), limitation of actions (2025 Mont. Laws ch. 174).
- **Item 13 (already done):** existing MT override rows, Taylor's 2026-11-12 decisions, MT log s6.1 options and s6.3 calls all stand; no existing MT row was edited.
- **Item 10 (binding-arbitration):** not offered. Title 27, ch. 5 read whole; Mont. Code Ann. § 28-2-708's last sentence saves agreements enforceable under that chapter, but whether a pre-dispute clause in a residential lease waives the ch. 24 court remedies (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-202(1)), whether a lease falls within Mont. Code Ann. § 27-5-114(2)(b)'s exclusion, and FEDERAL: 9 U.S.C. § 2 preemption all turn on case law, so Taylor's 2026-11-07 rule applies and `arbitration-agreement` is not tagged MT (its own notes already hold back states with a Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-403(2)-type penalty). The 2025 text of Mont. Code Ann. § 27-5-114 carries no first-page notice requirement. Row: see §2 `binding-arbitration`.
- **Item 2 (s18.2 and s6.1 conversions):** every s18.2 status and s6.1 option on a missing topic is now a row (battery-backed absence, barred term quoting the bar, or rule 54 declined-option row). Grounds the corpus did not support were corrected in the new rows and reported in §6: the jury-waiver bar (Justice and City Court Rules of Civil Procedure, Rule 15 bars nothing; Mont. Const. art. II, § 26 is the anchor), the homestead waiver (no tenant homestead, Mont. Code Ann. § 70-32-103(1); Mont. Code Ann. § 25-13-601 missed), and Mont. Code Ann. § 70-25-201(4) as the bar on collection and notice fees (it is Mont. Code Ann. § 28-2-721 and Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-442).
- **Item 3 (prohibited terms):** all 21 named topics answered in loads L01 and L02 (see §2).
- **Battery log note (check R1-B1):** L06-rent-demand-bar-3 supersedes both L06-rent-demand-bar-1 and L06-rent-demand-bar-2 (its supersedes field names only -1); the row's notes already say so. Recorded here rather than rewriting the log.

#### §4.2 Calls made without asking (rule 76)

- `accessory-dwelling-unit`: MLUPA local governments: by Mont. Code Ann. § 76-25-105(4) the ch. 2 ADU protections do not bind them; stated with the cautious course (follow local rules, take advice) rather than NEEDS_REVIEW, because the statute text is clear and the corpus is complete for statutes.
- `accessory-dwelling-unit`: Minimum rental term: no statute; 30-day local minimum fits the STR saving clause; longer minimum untested (case law) - cautious course stated.
- `actual-notice-method`: Declined a text-message/portal notice clause (rule 54): a deemed-notice term reads as a Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-202(1) waiver, so under the 2026-11-07 arbitration rule it is not offered; education row says it is lawful as a courtesy copy and why it is not offered.
- `alarm-tampering-fee`: Tagged the LA clause without a fault limit: Montana puts detector maintenance on the tenant and defines damage to include accidental acts (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-25-101(2)).
- `alt-housing`: Treated the essential-services substitute-housing remedy as the only Montana rehousing rule; no landlord duty.
- `animal-care-access`: Animal care framed as an agreed service under Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-312(1) with 24-hour notice kept; an animal left alone is not treated as a statutory emergency (definition protects the premises or the tenant).
- `animal-care-access`: Did not deem notice impracticable in advance (possible waiver of a chapter right).
- `appliances-excluded`: Offered a tenant-owned-appliance clause instead of an exclusion of landlord leftovers; leftovers must be listed as included or removed (strictest reading of Mont. Code Ann. § 70-25-101(3) with Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-303(1)(d)).
- `appliances-excluded`: Main pass after check round 1 (appliances-excluded-mt): fault carve-out added (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-202(3)), applying the same test as furnishings-included-mt (check R1-A).
- `asbestos-hazards`: Row is education (no lease clause): no Montana disclosure duty exists, and a clause reciting the Act would only restate law (rule 55).
- `asbestos-hazards`: NEEDS_REVIEW not used: the asbestos rules (not in corpus) govern how projects are permitted, not whether a landlord must disclose; the body bounds the claim and gives the cautious course.
- `asbestos-hazards`: Main pass after check round 1 (edu-asbestos-hazards-mt): $80,000 total cap added (Mont. Code Ann. § 75-2-515(4)(a); check R1-B2).
- `association-obligations-disclosure`: Applied Mont. Code Ann. § 70-23-601 to residential leases of condominium units on its words (strictest reading), as a REQUIRED_DISCLOSURE clause; no stated penalty.
- `balcony-inspection`: Annual and turnover inspection given as cautious practice, not as a legal duty.
- `bed-bug-cooperation`: Tag rather than a new Montana clause: pest-cooperation-pr routes entry to the Montana entry clause and its fault group matches Montana's fault exceptions; no Montana-specific text needed (rule 26).
- `bed-bug-cooperation`: Topic row requirement met by the tag (row keyed bed-bug-cooperation); the absence itself stays in edu-no-bed-bug-rule-mt (keyed bed-bug-disclosure), which stands.
- `binding-arbitration`: Decided under Taylor's 2026-11-07 rule without asking: the clause is not offered because whether it is a Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-202(1) term, whether a lease falls in the Mont. Code Ann. § 27-5-114(2)(b) $5,000 exclusion, and federal preemption all turn on case law; no proposal to tag arbitration-agreement MT.
- `binding-arbitration`: Mont. Code Ann. § 27-5-114 as codified in 2025 has no typed-on-first-page notice requirement (batteries L02-binding-arbitration-2, -3); the pre-1997 text was not read, so the row makes no claim about what the 1997 act removed.
- `carpet-cleaning`: Offered a Montana carpet clause (not a decline): a cleanliness standard with professional cleaning as the type the notice may name is lawful if the Mont. Code Ann. § 70-25-201(3) notice and 24 hours are kept and normal wear is excluded; no fixed or automatic charge (Taylor's instruction; MT log s6.1).
- `carpet-cleaning`: Did not adopt the Utah/Colorado no-labor rule: Mont. Code Ann. § 70-25-201(1) allows a reasonable charge for the landlord's labor; the clause still charges only the cost actually paid to a professional cleaner.
- `carpet-cleaning`: Main pass after check round 1 (edu-no-carpet-cleaning-rule-mt): presumption stated as a presumption; court-claim exception added (check R1-B1).
- `casualty-and-mitigation-waivable`: Treated the landlord mitigation duty (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-426(3)) as a tenant right or remedy under ch. 24, so a lease waiver is a Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-202(1) term (plain text; no case read).
- `casualty-and-mitigation-waivable`: Existing rows casualty-termination-mt and tenant-caused-damage-mt stand (Taylor); the new row only points to them.
- `children-occupancy`: Rule type PROHIBITED (the row states bars). No clause: Montana requires no lease text on children; Permitted Occupants (tagged) lists children as occupants.
- `children-occupancy`: Advised pegging any landlord cap to the applicable code (Taylor's instruction; FEDERAL: 42 U.S.C. § 3607(b)(1) protects only 'reasonable' government restrictions).
- `cold-weather-vacate-notice`: Offered an MT optional clause rather than tagging the WY clause: trigger moved to the statutory 7-day absence, entry limited to Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-426(2) and emergencies.
- `cold-weather-vacate-notice`: Temperature left as a hand-filled bracket (no builder variable).
- `collection-fee`: Answer to Taylor: a collection fee is not barred by ch. 24; it is lawful only as reimbursement of actual, reasonable costs (enforceability unsettled, case law), and a flat or percentage fee is void under Mont. Code Ann. § 28-2-721(1) unless actual damage is impracticable to fix. Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-442 governs fees in actions, not pre-suit charges. MT log s6.1 decline stands; the row says it is lawful but not offered and why.
- `collection-fee`: Main extra reason to decline: Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-103(14) makes any agreed charge rent, so the other-state models that keep the charge out of rent cannot be copied for Montana.
- `collection-fee`: Main pass after check round 1 (edu-no-collection-fee-rule-mt): 'lawful' replaced by 'not barred, enforceability unsettled' (check R1-B1).
- `common-area-lighting`: Basis SERVES_LANDLORD, not CONSTRAINED_TERM: no Montana law limits a figure in the clause.
- `common-area-lighting`: Group Rent & Payment (utility obligation); CONDITIONAL with an [Optional.] bracket.
- `common-area-lighting`: Did not copy the ND mid-term bar (a limit Montana lacks); left mid-term changes to rules-mt and the term-change rules.
- `condemned-premises-rent-bar`: Rent during unlawful occupancy: case law only; cautious course (stop charging, refund prepaid, advice) stated.
- `confession-of-judgment`: Answered the supersedeas sub-question from Mont. Code Ann. § 25-33-201 (statute), noting that Montana has no court rule on it.
- `confirmed-absences-habitability`: Treated the topic as a summary row like the GU model: each absence is backed by a battery run in this pass and points to its own row.
- `confirmed-absences-misc`: Main pass after check round 1 (edu-confirmed-absences-misc-mt): cross-reference to the plain-language row (check R1-B1).
- `confirmed-absences-outside-title`: Main pass after check round 1 (edu-confirmed-absences-outside-title-mt): contradiction with edu-rent-receipts-mt stated, not described as standing (check R1-B1 DEFECT); the existing row is reported for correction.
- `construction-liens`: Tagged no-liens-ar despite the Montana "agreed to the improvement" test: the clause is lawful and states no law; the gap is explained in the education row and MT segment (cautious course) rather than writing a near-duplicate Montana clause.
- `deposit-cost-schedule`: No battery: a present bar quoted from its own section (rule 19 applies to absences).
- `deposit-cost-schedule`: Reading of "penalties due under lease provisions, and other money owing": non-cleaning, non-damage lease charges deductible; cleaning and damage measured only as the section says (cautious, narrower reading).
- `deposit-cost-schedule`: Main pass after check round 1 (edu-deposit-cost-schedule-mt): bar restated as a reading with the cautious course; presumption wording (check R1-B1).
- `deposit-installments`: Installment option is lawful; declined as a separate clause because the tagged due-at-signing clause already holds a payment schedule (rule 54 education row).
- `deposit-installments`: Missed installment treated as a 14-day noncompliance, not rent (cautious course).
- `deposit-surrender-notice`: No battery: present rule quoted from its own sections; the "no definition of surrender" point rests on reading ch. 25 whole.
- `deposit-surrender-notice`: Main pass after check round 1 (edu-deposit-surrender-notice-mt): pending-court-claim exception added (check R1-B1; Mont. Code Ann. § 70-25-202(2)).
- `designated-repairer`: Declined a preferred-repairer clause (lawful but non-binding); consent-before-repair terms treated as barred waivers.
- `disability-accommodation`: Rule type REQUIRED (education row stating a duty).
- `disability-accommodation`: Restoration escrow: federal rule stated; Montana deposit presumption (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-25-101(4)) applied on the cautious course so the account also follows Montana's return rules.
- `disaster-duties`: Flood, wildfire and storm damage treated as "casualty" under Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-409 (undefined term; no case searched).
- `drug-free-housing-addendum`: Separate drug-free addendum declined as a lawful option (rule 54); criminal-activity-mt covers the subject.
- `drug-free-housing-addendum`: Cautious reading: a lease term breaching Mont. Code Ann. § 16-12-108(6) treated as a possible prohibited provision with penalty exposure.
- `dv-confidentiality`: Program rules (Mont. Code Ann. § 40-15-120) would be Department of Justice rules in ARM title 23 subchapters not in the corpus; the answer does not turn on them because the statute names who must accept the card (government agencies).
- `dv-deposit-timing`: Co-tenant deposit timing and division is a case-law point; cautious course (written agreement of all tenants; otherwise hold for the continuing tenancy) stated, row VERIFIED.
- `dv-deposit-timing`: Main pass after check round 1 (edu-no-dv-deposit-timing-mt): pending-court-claim exception added (check R1-B1).
- `dv-eviction-protection`: Federal VAWA stated only from the saved excerpt; covered-program limit stated in the body.
- `dv-eviction-protection`: Fair-housing (sex) angle is a case-law point; cautious course (take advice) stated, row VERIFIED.
- `dv-lockchange`: Co-tenant lockout on the strength of an order of protection: case-law point; cautious course (lock change only at the protected tenant request, no removal of belongings or termination without court process) stated, row VERIFIED.
- `dv-lockchange`: No optional lock-change clause offered: a clause granting permission restates Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-312(5) and serves the tenant; the body tells the landlord to give written permission.
- `dv-protection-order-chapter-moved`: Wrote a present-answer row (location unchanged) rather than "not applicable", per Taylor instruction; no battery for absence beyond the census, since the History lines and the session-law census settle that nothing moved.
- `electric-submetering-disclosure`: Landlord as public utility when reselling electricity or gas: not settled by any text read (Commission orders and case law not in the corpus); row VERIFIED with the cautious course (pass through actual cost only, no markup or fee, ask the Commission), per SOP Part 5.
- `electric-submetering-disclosure`: No lease clause offered: Montana requires no submetering disclosure (rule 55), and the library's only Montana billing clauses (utility-apportionment-mt, utility-allowance-cap-mt) exclude landlord-submetered electricity and gas.
- `emergency-contact`: Did not tag emergency-contact-co to avoid duplicating tenant-death-contact-mt (brief).
- `eminent-domain`: Tagged eminent-domain-vi rather than writing a Montana clause: generic text, true for Montana as written, and Montana absence confirmed by own battery.
- `entity-landlord-court-representation`: Where Mont. Code Ann. § 25-31-601(2) (any person may act as attorney) conflicts with Uniform Rule 14(b) and JCRCP Rule 2(a), the row states the court rules as the cautious course (no case law in corpus).
- `entity-landlord-court-representation`: Read Uniform Rule 14(c) (LLC majority member) as limited to justice court; cautious course in city court is a licensed attorney.
- `entity-landlord-court-representation`: Read Mont. Code Ann. § 30-13-215 as barring suit under the unregistered name only, not suit in the true name; cautious course is to register.
- `entity-landlord-court-representation`: Merged foreign-entity registration and assumed-name consequences (Taylor item 12) into this row rather than a second education row (one education row per topic).
- `entity-landlord-court-representation`: Main pass after check round 1 (edu-entity-landlord-court-representation-mt): LLC filing rule (Justice and City Court Rules of Civil Procedure, Rule 2(a), court rule) and cautious course stated in the body (check R1-B2 DEFECT).
- `entity-landlord-court-representation`: Main pass after check round 1 (edu-entity-landlord-court-representation-mt): majority-member exception kept for appearance (check round 2)
- `environmental-event-termination`: Read a sudden damaging natural event (flood, wildfire, storm) as a "casualty" under Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-409; stated as ordinary treatment, not a rule; no case searched.
- `ev-charging-end-of-tenancy`: no-alterations: not affected; Mont. Code Ann. § 70-18-101 and Mont. Code Ann. § 70-18-102 are the 'applicable law' its last sentence defers to, and its default matches Mont. Code Ann. § 70-18-101.
- `ev-charging-end-of-tenancy`: Integral-part question for hard-wired chargers left to the lease (case law only; cautious course stated).
- `ev-charging-requirements`: no-alterations: nothing found changes it. Montana has no EV statute; the 'applicable law' its last sentence defers to is general fixture law (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-18-101, agreement controls, else the affixed thing is the landowner's unless the owner requires removal; Mont. Code Ann. § 70-18-102, tenant may remove domestic-use fixtures during the term without injury unless integral), and its 'remain part of the property ... unless Landlord requires its removal' matches Mont. Code Ann. § 70-18-101.
- `ev-charging-requirements`: Wrote ev-charger-installation-mt rather than tagging ev-charger-installation-hi (open billing bracket; weights-and-measures condition would sit only in notes; deposit-cap wording).
- `ev-charging-requirements`: Weights and measures reach of a landlord submeter left unsettled in the row with the cautious course (tenant's own account, rent-inclusive, or ask the program); VERIFIED because the definition is statutory and the open point is application, not an unread source the answer turns on.
- `ev-charging-requirements`: Folded the end-of-tenancy election (from ev-charger-removal-restoration-nd's structure) into ev-charger-installation-mt so there is one clause, not two.
- `ev-charging-requirements`: Main pass after check round 1 (ev-charger-installation-mt): forfeiture election removed and the leave-in-place option reworded (check R1-A): Mont. Code Ann. § 70-18-102 lets the tenant remove a domestic-use fixture during the term, and an unremoved charger is handled as property left behind (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-430); additional-insured requirement replaced by notice of cancellation (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-202(3) cautious course); weights-and-measures condition put in the body.
- `ev-charging-requirements`: Main pass after check round 1 (ev-charger-installation-mt): only permanently attached parts pass to Landlord (check round 2)
- `ev-charging-shared-area`: no-alterations: not affected (no shared-area EV statute).
- `ev-charging-shared-area`: Damage sentence narrowed to Tenant's own side and carves out Landlord's negligence (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-202(3)).
- `ev-charging-shared-area`: Main pass after check round 1 (ev-charging-shared-area-mt): association-consent citation corrected (check R1-A).
- `eviction-hardship-stay`: Called a local eviction moratorium "very likely" preempted under Mont. Code Ann. § 7-1-111(13) rather than certainly; no case searched.
- `eviction-hardship-stay`: Left the interplay of Montana Rules of Civil Procedure, Rule 62(a) with the immediate writ in Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-427(4) unsettled in notes; the body does not rely on it.
- `eviction-penalty-clause-ban`: Called a fixed eviction fee "very likely void" (Mont. Code Ann. § 28-2-721) rather than barred outright; whether a particular amount meets (2) is fact-dependent.
- `eviction-penalty-clause-ban`: Noted that Mont. Code Ann. § 70-25-201(1) "penalties due under lease provisions" does not validate a penalty.
- `eviction-service-party`: Read a void lease appointment as not an agent "authorized by appointment" under JCRCP Rule 4D(2)(b)(i).
- `eviction-service-party`: For an address-confidentiality participant, recommended serving the tenant personally and also sending a copy to the Department of Justice (cautious course; statute silent on evictions).
- `exculpatory-clauses`: Listed Mont. Code Ann. § 27-1-1603 with its termination date; it is a single temporary section (no future version).
- `family-child-care`: Cautious course: paid family child care treated as a limited business under Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-321(1)(g) (consent not unreasonably withheld).
- `farmland-hunting-rights`: Wrote a Montana clause instead of tagging farmland-sporting-rights-co / hunting-rights-ms: their grant-to-tenant path and reserved landlord entry are conditional fits under Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-104(8) and Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-312.
- `farmland-hunting-rights`: Read reserved landlord hunting entry onto leased land as access governed by Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-312 (no case).
- `farmland-hunting-rights`: Main pass after check round 1 (hunting-rights-reserved-mt): reserved sporting entry moved off the leased area (check R1-A): Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-312 gives no access ground for hunting, so the reservation now covers only Landlord's land outside the leased area, and sporting use of the leased land needs Tenant's written consent.
- `farmland-hunting-rights`: Main pass after check round 1 (edu-farmland-hunting-rights-mt): aligned with the redrafted clause (check round 2)
- `fee-in-lieu-of-deposit`: Presumption treated as controlling (cautious course); rebuttal by a particular product is a case-law point.
- `fee-in-lieu-of-deposit`: Third-party bond or insurance products: insurance law not researched; body says take advice (cautious course), row VERIFIED because the Montana landlord-tenant answer does not turn on it.
- `fee-transparency`: Answered the auto-renewal question by pointing to Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-205 (the act's own continuation rule) rather than a consumer regulation; no Montana negative-option rule exists.
- `fee-transparency`: Recommended listing every fee in the lease as the cautious course under Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-103(14) and Mont. Code Ann. § 30-14-103; no clause offered (a fee schedule is the landlord's own content, and no Montana disclosure rule makes it REQUIRED).
- `fee-unprovided-service`: Stated Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-408 as the tenancy remedy where an unprovided service is an essential service required by the rental agreement or Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-303.
- `fire-code-standard`: Read Mont. Code Ann. § 50-61-103(6) family-residence exclusion with Admin. R. Mont. 23.12.401(25) and gave the cautious reading (rented house assumed covered); no case law in corpus.
- `fire-code-standard`: Declined a Montana tenant fire-safety clause (rule 54) because fire-safety-grilling, rules-mt and Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-321 cover it; decline stated in the body.
- `fire-code-standard`: Elevator registration/inspection folded into this row as part of the state building-safety codes reaching rentals (topic question), not split into its own row.
- `fire-sprinkler-duty`: Answered Present for new 5+ unit Group R buildings (Admin. R. Mont. 24.301.146(24)) rather than the first pass log line's "Confirmed absent for residential rentals", which read only the statute.
- `forfeiture-redemption`: Split redemption (late payment of rent) and forfeiture-redemption (relief against forfeiture generally) into two rows keyed to their own topics.
- `frozen-standard-incorporation`: rule_type REQUIRED (education row stating a duty).
- `frozen-standard-incorporation`: Stated that the code duty reaches only codes that applied to the building (Mont. Code Ann. § 50-60-102(1)(a) leaves small residential buildings outside the state code unless locally applied).
- `furnishings-included`: Added a fault limb in Mont. Code Ann. § 70-25-101(2) words and a pointer to the condition statement; any furniture deposit folded into the Security Deposit.
- `government-fee-reimbursement`: Offered the option although MT log s18.2 said "Not offered ... little use": that was a status line, not an MT log s6.1 decision, and Mont. Code Ann. § 7-13-2303(1)(c) per-dwelling-unit district fees give the clause real use; Taylor asked for tag-first on this row.
- `government-fee-reimbursement`: Did not tag the KY row because its example names a fee Montana local governments are unlikely to be able to charge (the Oklahoma treatment); wrote government-fee-reimbursement-mt.
- `government-fee-reimbursement`: Excluded property tax from this clause; property-tax pass-through is answered under tax-escalation.
- `government-fee-reimbursement`: Main pass: kept as an optional CONDITIONAL clause (never default). The s18.2 'Not offered' line was a status, not an s6.1 decision, and rule 54 requires offering a lawful option that serves the landlord; a per-dwelling-unit district fee (Mont. Code Ann. § 7-13-2303(1)(c)) gives it a real use.
- `governmental-fines`: Chose the damages variant (tenant-caused-fines-oh) over the reimbursement variant for Montana because of Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-202(3) and the Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-403(2) penalty; whether a reimbursement promise is an indemnity under Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-202(3) is case law not searched.
- `governmental-fines`: Main pass after check round 1 (tenant-caused-fines-oh): reasoning on Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-202(3) aligned with government-fee-reimbursement-mt (check R1-A).
- `governmental-fines`: Main pass after check round 1 (edu-no-governmental-fines-rule-mt): understatement of Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-422(5) corrected (check R1-B1).
- `guarantor-renewal`: Put advance authorization of renewals, rent changes, extra time and partial payment in the guaranty itself (Mont. Code Ann. § 28-11-211(1)); other changes need separate written consent.
- `guarantor-renewal`: Gave the guarantor a stated revocation for future renewal periods, matching Mont. Code Ann. § 28-11-108(2), rather than leaving revocability to case law.
- `guarantor-renewal`: Main pass: the guarantor-revocation sentence in guaranty-mt is kept. Mont. Code Ann. § 28-11-108(2) already lets a continuing guaranty 'be revoked at any time by the guarantor in respect to future transactions unless there is a continuing consideration'; stating the rule in the clause, limited to renewal periods beginning after written notice, matches the statute and lets the landlord decline to renew without a guarantor, so it was settled without asking.
- `habitability-materiality`: Read Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-406(2) and Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-407 as giving damages without the health-and-safety threshold; said so rather than presenting the threshold as a full materiality bar.
- `habitability-materiality`: Main pass after check round 1 (edu-habitability-materiality-mt): cautious course added (check R1-B1).
- `habitability-modifiable`: The (1)(f) "not required by law to be equipped" exception turns on model building codes not in the corpus; stated the cautious course (treat the duty as applying) rather than marking NEEDS_REVIEW, since the topic does not turn on it.
- `habitability-presumption`: MT log s18.2 said "Not located ... case law not searched"; the corpus battery now confirms the statutory absence, still bounded (case law not searched).
- `habitability-statement`: No optional habitability-statement clause offered: it would restate a duty the statute implies into every lease (rule 55: education, not a clause).
- `habitability-waiver`: Treated the (1)(f) "not required by law to be equipped" and tenant-controlled-installation exceptions as limits on the duty, not a waiver.
- `habitability-waiver`: Pointed to maintenance-allocation-mt for heat or water agreements rather than suggesting an in-lease (3) writing, following the MT log s6.3 strictest reading.
- `hazardous-contamination-disclosure`: Meth notice is not restated as a clause: it must precede agreement to the lease (Mont. Code Ann. § 75-10-1305(3)); edu-meth-disclosure-mt stands and this row points to it.
- `hazardous-contamination-disclosure`: Listing vs knowledge: the notice duty runs from the owner's knowledge and lack of remediation, not from the public list; listing usually supplies knowledge. No occupancy bar found in statute or rules.
- `hazardous-contamination-disclosure`: Optional institutional-control clause offered (rule 54) on a SERVES_LANDLORD basis tied to Mont. Code Ann. § 75-10-715(7)(c)(iv) and Mont. Code Ann. § 75-10-727(3).
- `health-district-rental-rules`: ARM Title 37 (DPHHS establishment rules) not in corpus: bounded in body; answer for ordinary rentals does not turn on it, so VERIFIED.
- `heating`: Two rows keyed heating (rule 57: one subject per row): the heat-supply duty and the boiler inspection law (topic question).
- `heating`: No new freeze-protection clause: L06 already offers cold-weather-precautions-mt; this row points to it.
- `heating`: Did not restate the (1)(f) "not required by law to be equipped" cautious reading in full; the pending edu-habitability-modifiable-mt carries it.
- `heating`: Main pass: edu-boiler-inspection-mt merged into edu-heating-mt (rule 57 bars two education rows on one topic for one state).
- `hoa`: Tagged association-approval-la: the Mont. Code Ann. § 70-17-901 grandfather limits whether an approval requirement binds the owner, which the clause already conditions on ("require"); the education row tells the landlord.
- `home-business`: No new clause: residential-use-only-mt already gives the Montana consent standard with a rules prompt.
- `homestead-waiver`: Did not compute a current homestead value: Mont. Code Ann. § 70-32-104(3)(a) leaves the figure to a Department of Revenue rule not in the corpus; stated the statutory base and escalator only.
- `homestead-waiver`: Read Mont. Code Ann. § 25-13-601 as limited to unsecured promissory notes on its words.
- `infirmity-termination`: Flagged a disability-based early release as a possible reasonable-accommodation request (Mont. Code Ann. § 49-2-305(5)(a)(ii)) on the cautious course; not stated as a right.
- `informal-dispute-resolution`: Followed the Oklahoma non-condition model because Montana shares the Field Code restraint-of-suit rule.
- `informal-dispute-resolution`: Main pass after check round 1 (informal-dispute-resolution-mt): response made a best-efforts step that is not a breach (check R1-A), so it cannot become a Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-422(1) noncompliance ground.
- `jury-waiver`: Rule type RECOMMENDED (not PROHIBITED): the corpus does not establish a bar; the clause is not offered under the arbitration rule because the answer turns on case law and possibly Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-202.
- `key-control-policy`: lock-change-ak declined for Montana (emergency five-day window departs from Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-312(5) and risks a Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-202(1) waiver reading); no Montana lock-change clause written because landlords-access-mt already carries the statutory sentence.
- `landlord-liability-insurance`: Stated the local-preemption point as a bar an insurance mandate would face, not as a settled holding.
- `landlord-remedies-termination`: Declined to offer a rent-acceleration option (no statute; mitigation duty and Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-201(2)(f) cap; enforceability only case law could settle); stated in the row body as the cautious course rather than as a separate declined-option row, since the option is not shown to be lawful.
- `landscaping-irrigation`: Kept MT log s6.3.1: shared clause not tagged; Montana answer is maintenance-allocation-mt.
- `late-fee-limit`: Answered the topic question on non-dwelling statutes: Montana has a self-storage late-fee safe harbour (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-6-606(2)) and no dwelling or lot counterpart; stated so the figure is not borrowed.
- `late-fee-limit`: Main pass after check round 1 (edu-no-late-fee-limit-mt): 'whichever is greater' restored (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-6-606; check R1-B1).
- `lead-safe-certification`: Main pass: federal lead text read (FEDERAL: 42 U.S.C. § 4852d(a)(1); FEDERAL: 40 C.F.R. § 745.113(b)); the "not read in this pass" caveat was removed from body and notes.
- `lead-state-notices`: Lease-option seller disclosure stated as a duty in the body (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-20-501(3), (9)); no lease clause offered because the statement is a separate seller document, not a lease term.
- `lead-state-notices`: Main pass: federal lead text read (FEDERAL: 42 U.S.C. § 4852d(a)(1); FEDERAL: 40 C.F.R. § 745.113(b)); the "not read in this pass" caveat was removed from body and notes.
- `lease-notice-initial-requirement`: A lease requiring the tenant to give more than the statutory 30/7 days is treated as a Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-202(1) waiver (cautious reading, no case).
- `lease-registration`: Recording is optional; whether possession is notice is case law, so the cautious course (record or give buyers a copy for leases over 1 year) is stated.
- `lease-term-limitation`: Whether a lease encumbers a married owner's homestead (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-32-301) is unsettled; body states the cautious course (both spouses sign and acknowledge).
- `lease-term-limitation`: Recording advice for leases over one year rests on Mont. Code Ann. § 70-21-301 and Mont. Code Ann. § 70-21-304 read together; case law on notice from possession not searched.
- `limitation-of-actions`: rule_type PROHIBITED because the row carries the Mont. Code Ann. § 28-2-708 bar on shortening clauses; the periods are stated as information.
- `limitation-of-actions`: Deposit claim period (2-year statutory liability vs 6-year written instrument) left to case law; cautious course stated (keep records 6 years).
- `limitation-of-actions`: Read the Title 27, ch. 5 saving in Mont. Code Ann. § 28-2-708 as reaching the forum restriction only.
- `liquidated-damages`: Read Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-201(2)(f) as the only tenancy-specific agreed-damages rule; the early-termination amount is the statute's own allowance, not a Mont. Code Ann. § 28-2-721(2) exception.
- `lockout-for-rent-delinquency`: Barred row (PROHIBITED) keyed to lockout-for-rent-delinquency; an opt-in lockout clause is a Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-202(1) waiver, so no clause is offered and the Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-403(2) penalty is stated.
- `lockout-for-rent-delinquency`: Read Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-113 (unauthorized persons) as not reaching a tenant holding a rental agreement, by its own (2)(a) definition of authorization.
- `maintenance-duty-shift`: Followed MT log s6.3.1 as instructed; no new legal call.
- `meter-conservation-charge`: Treated the universal system benefits charge as related but not a meter conservation charge: it funds programs generally and is not repayment of improvements at a particular premises.
- `military-air-zone-disclosure`: Education row only: the zoning duties bind local governments, owners and builders, and no statute gives a disclosure or a termination right; cautious disclosure stated as practice.
- `minor-tenant-filing`: Stated the fair-housing age point cautiously (no statutory exception for minors; case law not searched): treat minor applicants on the same objective criteria and take advice before treating them differently.
- `nonrefundable-deposit-notice`: Barred row (PROHIBITED); no lease clause for non-deposit charges offered here (fees-as-rent and application-fee rows cover them).
- `nonresident-owner-agent`: Treated pass-through entity composite/withholding (Mont. Code Ann. § 15-30-3313) as the only state reporting duty touching nonresident rental owners; federal 1099 reporting left out of scope.
- `notice-service-fee`: Answer to Taylor: a notice-service fee is lawful only at actual cost (and then largely duplicates recoverable damages under Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-401(1)); a flat fee is void under Mont. Code Ann. § 28-2-721(1) unless impracticable to fix; eviction costs in an action are for the court under Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-442(1). Not barred by ch. 24; MT log s6.1 decline stands.
- `notice-service-fee`: Main pass after check round 1 (edu-no-notice-service-fee-mt): enforceability stated as unsettled; e-mail completion rule of Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-108(1)(c) added (check R1-B1).
- `optional-lease-terms`: Option-to-purchase clause declined (rule 54): a real estate sale contract with its own disclosure regime; education given in this index row.
- `optional-lease-terms`: Index lists answers saved at writing time; assembler should refresh the gaps-pass lists (fees-and-charges-mt and condominium-unit-lease-contents-mt excluded as required rows).
- `optional-lease-terms`: Main pass after check round 1 (edu-optional-lease-terms-mt): waterbed-mn removed from the shared-clause list and waterbed-mt added to the Montana list (the tag was replaced after check round 1).
- `other-landlord-facilities`: Private-residence exclusion (Mont. Code Ann. § 50-60-704(3)) not read as settling whether a rented single-family house is covered; body advises checking with the department.
- `owner-move-in-reservation`: Reserved owner-move-in termination treated as a termination without cause under Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-201(2)(f) (coordinator instruction): tenant's damages capped at 1 month's rent; clause fixes the agreed amount.
- `owner-move-in-reservation`: Agreed amount floor 'more than $0' (cautious: a zero amount could be read as a Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-202(1) waiver).
- `owner-move-in-reservation`: Clause expressly carves out early-termination-mt's sentence limiting landlord early termination.
- `owner-move-in-reservation`: Basis CONSTRAINED_TERM (agreed amount limited by statute), rule_type CONSTRAINED.
- `owner-move-in-reservation`: Main pass: the floor (an agreed amount of more than $0, not more than one month's rent) is kept, settled without asking. Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-201(2)(f) caps the agreed amount at 1 month's rent but does not set a floor; a $0 figure would read as giving up the tenant's damages remedy, which a rental agreement may not do (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-202(1)), so the cautious course keeps a positive amount.
- `parking-rules-notice`: No new parking clause: rules-mt already states Montana's rule conditions and notice periods; this row is education plus pointers.
- `parking-rules-notice`: Local towing ordinances: preemption under Mont. Code Ann. § 7-1-111(13) judged doubtful for towing (not an activity with regard to tenants as such); row says check local ordinances.
- `part5-nonwaivable`: Applied the act to a seller-lessee by reading Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-104(2) as excluding only a purchaser in possession; no case searched.
- `periodic-services-entry`: Standing schedule at signing not treated as notice of each later entry (unsettled; cautious per-visit notice).
- `periodic-services-entry`: Hour window bracket narrows reasonable times only; 24 hours fixed in text, not a bracket.

- `pest-control-notice`: Public-housing reach of Mont. Code Ann. § 80-8-107 left to the text's own examples with the cautious course (case law only could settle "open to the public").
- `plain-language-consumer-statement`: Treat every residential lease as within the Plain Language in Contracts Act (value test and real-estate-transfer exclusion unsettled); cautious course in the body.
- `plain-language-consumer-statement`: Product note: generated leases must stay captioned by section, in readable type and contrasting ink (Mont. Code Ann. § 30-14-1103(2)); the library drafting already aims at plain language.
- `political-access`: Political signs left to the existing row (MT log s6.3.10 stands); this row covers canvasser access and points to it.
- `pool-safety`: Treated a pool at a multi-unit rental as a public swimming pool (no exclusion fits: not used only by the owner, family or invited guests).
- `pool-safety`: Did not add rent-abatement or "use at own risk" language to pool-use-mt (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-202(1), (3)).
- `pool-safety`: Main pass, on L08's pool question: settled without asking. Whether a pool at a single-family rental is a 'public' pool turns on the words of Mont. Code Ann. § 50-53-102(7)(b)(i) (owner, owner's family and invited guests), which a tenant may not fit; no case or rule in the corpus settles it, so the row states both readings and the cautious course and stays VERIFIED (SOP Part 5). The health department's pool rules (not in the corpus) are a sync item, not a blocker, because the definition is statutory.
- `pool-safety`: Main pass after check round 1 (pool-use-mt): age-keyed supervision rule replaced with an age-neutral rule (check R1-A DEFECT): Montana's housing discrimination section covers age and familial status with no reasonable-grounds exception (Mont. - `pool-safety`: Main pass after check round 1 (pool-use-mt): existing {{pool_guest_limit}} variable restored (check round 2)

Code Ann. § 49-2-305(5)(a)(i)), already in its MT segment.
- `portable-cooling-device`: Rule 54 cooling clause declined (education row states it is lawful and why not offered).
- `portable-solar`: no-alterations: no Montana statute gives a tenant (or an owner against an association) a solar-device right, and the solar easement statute (Mont. Code Ann. §§ 70-17-301 to 70-17-302) creates none (battery L09-portable-solar-1), so nothing affects the shared no-alterations clause; its deferral to applicable law reaches only the disability modification right. FEDERAL: 47 C.F.R. § 1.4000 does not reach solar devices.
- `portable-solar`: Rule 54 solar clause declined (education row states it is lawful and why not offered).

- `portfolio-thresholds`: Building-code thresholds included briefly because they turn on unit counts a landlord controls; model codes not read.
- `private-well-testing`: Cautious course (test before each tenancy, disclose known unsafe water) stated as practice, not law.
- `prohibited-acts-renter`: No new prohibited-acts clause: each limb is already in an MT clause; a new one would duplicate them (rule 28). Education row indexes them.
- `promises-to-repair`: No Montana optional clause offered: a promised-repairs schedule has no Montana basis under rule 55 (not a required disclosure, no constrained figure, serves the tenant); the education row gives the cautious course.

- `protected-class-inquiry-ban`: Montana's inquiry ban is purpose-limited; the row says so and gives the cautious course rather than implying a blanket ban.

- `rent-receipt-anti-waiver`: Taylor's pointer to Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-424 was checked: Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-424 is refusal of access; the acceptance-of-rent waiver rule is Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-423.
- `rent-reporting`: Bounded the claim to rent-reporting rules; stated the one Montana rule that reaches a reporting landlord (Mont. Code Ann. § 31-3-141(3)) and that Mont. Code Ann. § 31-3-131(1) by its words does not reach rental decisions.

### §5 Independent check (rule 80, scaled: at most 2 rounds, one agent at a time)

Checkers were separate agents that had not written the rows, briefed by `CHECK-BRIEF.md` (clauses that take something from the tenant and the Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-403(2) exposure; tags; every figure and period; every quote; absence batteries and spot reruns; contradictions; the struck "for actual damages"; citation format; cautious course). They read the snapshot `check/pre-R1.csv`, the corpus section-open, the battery logs and the saved federal texts, and edited nothing.

**Round 1** ran in three sequential slices over all 246 rows:
- Slice A, the 24 Montana clauses and 16 tags: 1 DEFECT, 11 IMPRECISION, 6 NOTE. Worst: an age-keyed pool supervision rule (`pool-use-mt`, Mont. Code Ann. § 49-2-305(1)); a charger forfeiture that bypassed Mont. Code Ann. § 70-18-102 and Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-430 (`ev-charger-installation-mt`); `tenant-portal-mt` against the tagged `acceptable-payment-methods`. All quotes matched; every figure matched; every battery existed and passed.
- Slice B1, 101 education rows: 2 DEFECT, 16 IMPRECISION, 3 NOTE. Worst: a pointer to a withdrawn clause (`edu-rent-escalation-mt`); the receipt contradiction with the existing `edu-rent-receipts-mt` (F-10); the fee presumption stated as fixed law in five rows. 772 quotes, 0 misses; 11 everyday-word spot reruns, no absence reversed.
- Slice B2, 105 education rows: 1 DEFECT, 6 IMPRECISION, 7 NOTE. Worst: the LLC filing rule (`edu-entity-landlord-court-representation-mt`); rent-to-reletting given to "either side" (`edu-no-seasonal-tenancy-rule-mt`); deposit exceptions omitted in three rows. 8 spot reruns, no absence reversed; one figure omission (the $80,000 asbestos cap).

Every round-1 finding was acted on; 46 rows were edited (confirmed by diff against the snapshot, `check/R2-scope.json`). The waterbed-mn tag was replaced by Montana's own `waterbed-mt` with a fault carve-out.

**Round 2** re-checked only those 46 rows (before/after diff against the round-1 finding): 23 CURED, 3 DEFECT, 13 IMPRECISION, 11 NOTE (three of them on rows outside the scope, found through contradictions with edited rows). The DEFECTs were stale text left beside a fix: `edu-farmland-hunting-rights-mt` still described the old hunting entry, and `edu-tenant-records-mt` and `edu-no-property-tax-rent-statement-mt` still said Montana has no receipt rule. No clause's operative text uses a Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-202 term, and no quote mismatched. Every round-2 finding was acted on, except the ones that are sync work on existing rows (F-10 on `edu-rent-receipts-mt`; the acceptable-payment-methods MT segment line), which are in §6.

### §6 Findings on existing rows

Nothing was edited.

- F-1 security-deposit-return-mt (bodyText): 'Landlord has a pending claim for actual damages filed in court'. Mont. Code Ann. § 70-25-202(2) as codified after 2025 Mont. Laws ch. 656, § 2: 'This section does not apply if a rental agreement is terminated pursuant to 70-24-427 or 70-33-427 and the landlord has a pending claim filed in court.' Fix: 'and Landlord has a pending claim filed in court'. The row's notes already record the discrepancy; MT log s6.3.7 chose the narrower words so the clause would never excuse a deadline the code would not, but the code is now confirmed, so the clause understates the exception (it never misleads the tenant to their harm, which is why this is a wording fix, not a defect).
- F-2 tenant-caused-damage-mt: for damage by fire or casualty its fault set (act OR OMISSION, of a guest OR ANY OTHER PERSON on the premises with consent) is wider than Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-409(2) ('the purposeful or negligent act of the tenant or the tenant's family or guests'), and 'guest' is defined narrowly (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-103(8): 'a person staying with a tenant for a temporary period of time'). Where a fire is caused by a negligent omission, or by a consenting visitor who is not staying with the tenant, term (1) 'Rent will not abate' would take away the tenant's Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-409(1)(b) proportional rent reduction: a waiver of a remedy under ch. 24 (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-202(1)), unenforceable (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-403(1)), with knowing-use exposure (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-403(2)). The row's notes say every abatement right it touches 'exclude[s] tenant-caused conditions', which is true for Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-406 and Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-408 but not in these words for Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-409. Proposed fix: add after the opening condition: "For damage by fire or casualty, this Section applies only where the damage was caused by the purposeful or negligent act of Tenant, Tenant's family or Tenant's guests."
- F-3 edu-servicemember-mt: ends 'Other servicemembers should check federal law, which this library doesn't cover.' The library does cover federal law (FEDERAL: prefix rows), and FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955 applies to every servicemember lease in Montana. Proposed addition: "Federal law separately lets any servicemember end a residential lease on entering military service, or on permanent-change-of-station or deployment orders for 90 days or more, by written notice with a copy of the orders; for monthly rent, termination takes effect 30 days after the next rent due date after the notice, and you may not charge an early termination fee (FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955)."
- F-4 edu-nonpayment-notice-mt, edu-noncompliance-cure-mt, edu-dangerous-activity-eviction-mt: state-law move-out periods stated without the federal overlay. Proposed addition to each: "If the property has a federally backed mortgage or takes part in a covered federal housing program, federal law also bars requiring the tenant to vacate until 30 days after a notice to vacate (see the CARES Act row)." And edu-eviction-process-mt's notes line 'Federal CARES Act notice not read' can be replaced by a pointer to edu-cares-act-notice.
- F-5 edu-smoke-co-detectors-mt (item 7 detector-rule read): no statement in the row is wrong, but its notes say 'The department rules (Admin. R. Mont.) on placement were not read', and the body omits what they require. Admin. R. Mont. 23.12.406(1): 'either an approved smoke alarm or smoke detector, or both, shall be installed by the landlord in each dwelling unit rented to another person, in accordance with the provisions of Section 907 of the IFC' (the IFC is adopted by reference and is not in the corpus). Admin. R. Mont. 24.320.301(1)(a): 'In dwelling units containing fuel-fired appliances or having attached garages, a carbon monoxide detector must be installed outside of each separate sleeping area in the immediate vicinity of the bedrooms'; (1)(b) single-station detectors must comply with UL 2034 and the manufacturer's instructions; (2) the rules 'may only be enforced as provided in Title 70, chapter 24, part 4'. The rule sets placement only for units with fuel-fired appliances or an attached garage; the statute (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-303(1)(g)) still says 'each dwelling unit', so the row's every-unit statement is the cautious reading and should stay. Proposed addition: "Place the carbon monoxide detector outside each separate sleeping area, near the bedrooms, in any unit with fuel-burning appliances or an attached garage, using a UL 2034 detector installed to the maker's instructions; the smoke alarm must meet the fire code's section 907."
- F-6 hoa-compliance (MT segment): accurate; could add that association rules the lease makes binding should be given to the tenant in writing, because Montana enforces landlord rules only with notice and in writing (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-311(1)(f), (2)). Optional wording, not an override.
- F-7 edu-plain-language-mt (from L13, verified by the main pass): the row says Montana has no plain-language lease law. The Plain Language in Contracts Act does reach a residential lease by a landlord who 'regularly sells, lets, or lends in connection with consumer contracts' (Mont. Code Ann. § 30-14-1102(4)): a 'Consumer contract' includes an agreement for the 'lease ... of ... property ... primarily for personal, family, or household purposes' (Mont. Code Ann. § 30-14-1102(3)), and it 'must be written in plain language' (Mont. Code Ann. § 30-14-1103(1)): 'clear and coherent', 'appropriately divided and captioned by its various sections', 'type of readable size', 'ink that contrasts with the paper' (Mont. Code Ann. § 30-14-1103(2)). Remedy: '$50 plus any actual damages' and costs (Mont. Code Ann. § 30-14-1111(1)). Scope exclusions: value over $50,000 and 'a transfer of real estate' (Mont. Code Ann. § 30-14-1104(2)(a), (f)); whether either reaches a lease is unsettled. New row edu-plain-language-contracts-act-mt carries the rule; retire or correct edu-plain-language-mt at sync. PRODUCT NOTE for the builder: a Montana lease generated by the app should keep captioned sections and readable type, since the Act applies to the document itself.
- F-8 extended-absence-notice-ks (tagged MT; from check round 1): its remedy sentence reaches only a "willfully" failed absence notice, but Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-426(1) lets the landlord 'recover actual damages from the tenant' whenever the rental agreement requires the notice and the tenant 'fails to do so', with no willfulness element. Narrower than Montana law and in the tenant's favour, so not a defect; the MT segment could say the statute's remedy is wider. cold-weather-precautions-mt tracks the statute for freeze damage.
- F-9 security-deposit-return-mt (from check round 1, adding to F-1): besides 'for actual damages', its body says the deadline exception applies where 'Landlord has brought an action for possession'; Mont. Code Ann. § 70-25-202(2) turns on the rental agreement being 'terminated pursuant to 70-24-427' and the landlord having 'a pending claim filed in court'. Fix both together: "These time limits do not apply if this Lease has been terminated under the statute on actions for possession and Landlord has a pending claim filed in court."
- F-10 edu-rent-receipts-mt (raised by L02, made a DEFECT by check round 1 because the library would contradict itself): body says 'Montana has no statute requiring a landlord to give receipts for rent, including cash rent'. Mont. Code Ann. § 28-1-1111: 'Whoever pays money or delivers an instrument or property is entitled to a written receipt therefor from the person to whom the payment or delivery is made'. Fix: "Montana has no landlord-specific receipt or rent-ledger statute, but its general law entitles anyone who pays money to a written receipt from the person paid, and the payer may demand a proper signature on it (Mont. Code Ann. § 28-1-1111)." Two new rows (edu-confirmed-absences-outside-title-mt, edu-rent-receipt-anti-waiver-mt) now say this row does not stand as written; fix it at sync before release so the library does not contradict itself.
- F-11 edu-no-ev-charging-rule-mt (existing master row; from check round 1): says Montana 'imposes no EV duty on landlords', while the new edu-ev-charging-requirements-mt finds three state laws that can reach charging a landlord provides (the public charging station tax rules, Mont. Code Ann. §§ 15-70-801 to 15-70-805 as amended by 2025 Mont. Laws ch. 216, for chargers over 25 kW not at a residence; Mont. Code Ann. § 69-8-803(3); weights and measures). Fix at sync: "imposes no general EV duty on landlords (a high-power charger tenants pay to use can be a public charging station; see edu-ev-charging-requirements-mt)", or retire it in favour of the new rows.
- F-12 retire-or-point at sync (supersedes convention): edu-plain-language-mt should be retired or pointed to edu-plain-language-contracts-act-mt (F-7). This pass left `supersedes` blank on new education rows that contradict existing master rows, because rule 26 forbids editing existing MT rows; the sync should set is_active FALSE on the old row or set the new row's supersedes. Clause rows that are Montana versions of untagged shared clauses keep the first pass's convention: three name their parent (carpet-cleaning-mt, fees-and-charges-mt, government-fee-reimbursement-mt), others leave it blank; the sync can apply either convention uniformly.
- F-13 acceptable-payment-methods (tagged MT; from check round 2): it lets 'Landlord ... change the accepted payment methods on reasonable written notice to Tenant'. Where tenant-portal-mt is used, that Section now controls and keeps a non-portal method for the whole tenancy. At sync append to the MT segment: "Where the Tenant Portal Section (tenant-portal-mt) is used, it controls and keeps a non-portal method for the whole tenancy; do not change methods to portal-only, since a portal account needs an e-mail address (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-202(4))."
- Release blockers for sync (from the check): F-10 (edu-rent-receipts-mt) and F-1/F-9 (security-deposit-return-mt) should be fixed before this delta ships, because new rows now state the corrected law and the library would otherwise contradict itself; F-7/F-12 (retire or point edu-plain-language-mt) likewise.
- (from `association-obligations-disclosure`) MT first-pass log s18.2 line for hoa says no rental-approval or rental-ban statute; Mont. Code Ann. § 70-23-601 (required contents of a lease of a unit) was known to battery 188 only as a failed positive and no row carries it. Not an existing-row error; new row added here.
- (from `collection-fee`) MT log s6.1 (not a row): it lists collection and notice fees as "Barred or unsupported" citing Mont. Code Ann. § 70-25-201(4); that subsection bars deductions only "for purposes other than those set forth in this section", and Mont. Code Ann. § 70-25-201(1) includes "other money owing to the landlord at the time of deduction", so (4) does not bar an agreed charge. The decisive rules are Mont. Code Ann. § 28-2-721 and Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-442. Proposed: reword the s6.1 entry to "lawful only at actual cost; declined" when the log is next synced.
- (from `confirmed-absences-outside-title`) edu-rent-receipts-mt: body says 'Montana has no statute requiring a landlord to give receipts for rent, including cash rent' (and its notes call receipt hits tax, banking and notice sections), but Mont. Code Ann. § 28-1-1111 provides 'Whoever pays money or delivers an instrument or property is entitled to a written receipt therefor from the person to whom the payment or delivery is made and may demand a proper signature to such receipt as a condition of the payment or delivery.' Proposed fix: add 'Montana's general law entitles anyone who pays money, including rent, to a written receipt from the person paid, and the payer may demand a signed receipt as a condition of paying (Mont. Code Ann. § 28-1-1111); a receipt for later rent is presumed to show earlier rent was paid (Mont. Code Ann. § 26-1-602(10)).' Not edited (existing rows stand).
- (from `deposit-surrender-notice`) security-deposit-return-mt: body ends "Landlord has a pending claim for actual damages filed in court"; codified Mont. Code Ann. § 70-25-202(2) reads 'the landlord has a pending claim filed in court' (2025 Mont. Laws ch. 656 struck "for actual damages"). Its notes say the narrower wording is deliberate (never excuses the deadlines where the code would not) and flag it for the legal watch. Proposed fix if Taylor wants code wording: replace "a pending claim for actual damages filed in court" with "a pending claim filed in court". Not edited.
- (from `dv-eviction-protection`) edu-no-dv-termination-mt: boundary says ARM not searched and its everyday battery had 0 real positives; L03-dv-eviction-protection-1/-2/-4 (whole corpus incl. ARM subchapters and 2025 session laws, real positives) confirm its absence finding. No fix proposed; the row stands.
- (from `electric-submetering-disclosure`) edu-no-utility-billing-rule-mt: body says "Montana has no statute on submetering"; still true of the statute book, but its boundary excluded Admin. R. Mont. and so it misses Admin. R. Mont. 38.5.1602 ('All electricity delivered to a new building shall be sold by the electric utility on the basis of individual meter measurement for each occupancy unit') and the Commission tenant-notice rules (Admin. R. Mont. 38.5.1405(2)(c)-(d)). Not wrong; proposed: add a cross-reference to edu-electric-submetering-disclosure-mt and edu-utility-landlord-account-mt at the next MT sync.
- (from `fee-transparency`) edu-payment-method-fee-mt: cites only Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-201(4); 2025 Mont. Laws ch. 768 added the same bar to the mobile home lot act ('A landlord may not charge an additional fee based on rent payment type except to recoup an electronic bank fee incurred for electronic payment.', Mont. Code Ann. § 70-33-201(4)). Not wrong; proposed: add the ch. 33 cite to its notes at the next MT sync.
- (from `fire-sprinkler-duty`) MT log s18.2 line for fire-sprinkler-duty (a log line, not a row) says "Confirmed absent for residential rentals"; the held rules show a new-construction requirement for Group R buildings with "5 or more dwelling units" (Admin. R. Mont. 24.301.146(24)). edu-smoke-co-detectors-mt is accurate as written (one- and two-unit bar only); no row fix proposed.
- (from `hoa`) MT first-pass log s18.2 (hoa line) says "no rental-approval or rental-ban statute"; Mont. Code Ann. § 70-17-901 (2019) limits association rental restrictions to owners who acquired subject to them or agreed in writing (applied to condominium associations by Mont. Code Ann. § 70-23-507). No existing row is wrong (hoa-compliance MT segment speaks only of the tenant duty); the log line should be read with edu-hoa-mt.
- (from `homestead-waiver`) No MT row is affected. MT log s6.1 / s18.2 cite Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-202(1) for the homestead-waiver bar; the exemptions are not rights "under this chapter", so that ground does not hold on the section’s words; Mont. Code Ann. § 25-13-601 (missed by the first pass) is the relevant statute. Proposed log wording: "Not offered: a tenant has no homestead in the rental (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-32-103(1)); no statutory waiver mechanism; exemption waivers in unsecured notes unenforceable (Mont. Code Ann. § 25-13-601)".
- (from `jury-waiver`) No MT row states the jury-waiver bar; the MT log s6.1 line ("Barred or unsupported: a jury waiver (Mont. Code Ann. § 28-2-708; Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-202(1); Montana Justice and City Court Rules of Civil Procedure, Rule 15)") and the s18.2 jury-waiver status overstate the grounds: Justice and City Court Rules of Civil Procedure, Rule 15 provides for demand and lists three ways a jury "may be waived" (docket consent, failure to demand, failure to appear) and bars nothing; Mont. Code Ann. § 28-2-708 and Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-202(1) do not reach a jury waiver on their words. Proposed fix: log wording "Not offered: no statute or rule authorizes or bars it; Mont. Const. art. II, § 26 allows waiver only in the manner the law provides; turns on case law (edu-jury-waiver-mt)". The outcome (no clause) is unchanged.
- (from `lead-safe-certification`) lead-based-paint (MT segment): says the only Montana lead reference is the seller disclosure "on a transfer of residential real property (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-20-502(1)(i))"; accurate, but Mont. Code Ann. § 70-20-501(3) defines "Contract" to include 'a lease with an option to purchase real property', so the seller disclosure (with its lead-based paint line) reaches a lease-option, and Admin. R. Mont. 24.210.641(1)(x) also exists. Proposed: add one sentence to the MT segment noting the lease-option reach; no change to the clause.
- (from `notice-service-fee`) MT log s6.1: same note as on collection-fee (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-25-201(4) is not the operative bar).
- (from `plain-language-consumer-statement`) edu-plain-language-mt: body says "Montana has no plain-language or readability statute for residential leases"; Mont. Code Ann. § 30-14-1103(1) 'A consumer contract must be written in plain language.' and Mont. Code Ann. § 30-14-1102(3) covers 'an agreement for the sale, lease, or loan of money, property, or services primarily for personal, family, or household purposes'. Proposed fix: reword the body to say Montana's Plain Language in Contracts Act applies (cautiously) to residential leases and point to edu-plain-language-contracts-act-mt; keep the Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-430(9) sentence.
- (from `rent-reporting`) edu-tenant-screening-mt: says Montana has no "state rule on ... adverse-action notices"; Mont. Code Ann. § 31-3-131(1) imposes an adverse-action duty on users of consumer reports but only for "credit or insurance for personal, family, or household purposes or employment", so the row is right for rentals as written; optional: add that Title 31, ch. 3 exists and does not reach rental decisions by its words, and that Mont. Code Ann. § 31-3-141(3) applies to anyone furnishing false information. Not an error; no fix required.
- (from `security-deposit-standards`) edu-no-deposit-holding-rule-mt: says a licensed property manager's trust-account duties come from board rules 'which this library does not cover'; the corpus now holds Admin. R. Mont. 24.210.427 (brokers: trust account, three business days, monthly reconciliation). Proposed addition to its notes or a pointer: see edu-no-security-deposit-standards-mt. Not edited.

### Proposed SOP changes

- (`main`) Rule 26: put in every helper brief, in these words, that a SINGLE-STATE row whose notes or basis cite its own state's statute is never tagged, even when the wording fits; copy the wording into the new state's own row. Two Utah rows were tagged by a helper in this pass and converted by the main pass, and a third tag (waterbed-mn) was replaced after the independent check for a fault carve-out.
- (`main`) Rule 57: the assembler should refuse a second row of the same content type on one topic for the new state (the check now runs in this pass's assemble.py); one helper wrote two education rows on `heating`.
- (`main`) Federal channel: where the shell and WebFetch cannot reach uscode.house.gov or ecfr.gov, the built-in browser can (2026-11-14: uscode.house.gov section pages at /us/usc/t<title>/s<section>, its old view.xhtml links return 404; eCFR at /current/title-N/section-X). Save excerpts to a federal folder the helpers can read, and have the quote checker search it.
- (`main`) Index rows (optional lease terms; required disclosures) should be written after every clause-writing load has finished, or topped up by script at assembly; here the optional-terms index was written before two later loads and needed one addition.
- (`main`) Independent check: give the round-1 checker of education rows the pass's own settled phrasings (deposit exceptions, 'presumed to be a security deposit', the e-mail completion rule of the notice statute) as a checklist; the same five omissions recurred across helpers and were caught only by the check.

### Proposed topic questions

- (`fire-code-standard`) Louisiana title move: Montana did not move its building code in 2025 (Title 50, ch. 60 stands; 2025 Mont. Laws ch. 368 amended Mont. Code Ann. § 50-60-203 only).
- (`hoa`) Does a statute protect a renter home child care against the association but not against the landlord? Montana: yes in effect (Mont. Code Ann. § 70-17-216 covenants; lease and Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-321(1)(g) govern landlord-tenant).
- (`tenant-bankruptcy`) Should tenant-bankruptcy get one shared federal education row (like edu-cares-act-notice) once 11 U.S.C. § 362(l)-(m) and § 365(d) are read? Recommendation: yes; per-state rows would then only state the state-law absence.
- (`tenant-bankruptcy`) (from L06, recast by the main pass as library work, not a Montana question) Tenant bankruptcy is wholly federal: consider one shared FEDERAL: row on the automatic stay and its two residential-eviction exceptions (11 U.S.C. § 362(b)(22)-(23), (l), (m)) tagged to every state, in the pattern of edu-cares-act-notice, with each state's own row saying only whether its law lets a tenant cure after judgment.

### Federal requests outstanding

- `asbestos-hazards`: FEDERAL: 40 C.F.R. §§ 61.141, 61.145 (asbestos NESHAP definitions and renovation/demolition notification; would let the row say whether a residential building of four or fewer units is outside the federal notice rule). Optional; row is written on the cautious course.
- `communal-land-lease-approval`: FEDERAL: 25 U.S.C. § 415 and FEDERAL: 25 C.F.R. part 162 (approval of leases of Indian trust and restricted land; residential leases), if the main pass wants the federal rule stated rather than flagged.
- `disability-accommodation`: FEDERAL: 24 C.F.R. Part 8 (Section 504, cost of modifications in federally assisted housing): flagged in the body without a rule statement; optional for the main pass.
- `dv-lockchange`: FEDERAL: 34 U.S.C. § 12491(e) (emergency transfers) was not in the saved excerpt; not needed for this row, which says only that the excerpt read gives no lock-change right.
- `eminent-domain`: FEDERAL: 42 U.S.C. § 4601 et seq. (Uniform Relocation Act) if the main pass wants the relocation-payment point stated beyond Mont. Code Ann. § 70-31-311.
