## Federal row: disposing of screening reports (Claude Code, 2026-11-20)

Taylor's decision 2026-11-20 (Washington gaps log §7, backlog item 9): one federal education row, `edu-fcra-disposal-rule`, is tagged here and in every jurisdiction, read from the official text (16 C.F.R. part 682 on eCFR; 15 U.S.C. § 1681w on govinfo.gov). It says that anyone who keeps a screening, credit or background report, or a record taken from one, for a business purpose must dispose of it with reasonable measures against unauthorized access (16 C.F.R. § 682.3), with the rule's examples, and that the rule sets no retention period (§ 682.4). This state's existing rows stay; no clause text changed.

## Shared edit: lead-based paint agent's statement (Claude Code, 2026-11-20)

From the West Virginia gaps sync (WV gaps log §6 item 21 and §7; backlog items from the Nevada and Puerto Rico syncs): the shared `lead-based-paint` clause gained a bracketed agent's statement and a sentence requiring each person who makes a statement to sign and date it, the two elements the federal rule requires in every lease of pre-1978 housing (40 C.F.R. § 745.113(b)(5)-(6); HUD's twin rule, 24 C.F.R. § 35.92(b)(5)-(6); both read on eCFR 2026-11-20). Uniform federal edit under rule 62: the same in every jurisdiction, no state term changed; it applies only where an agent acts for the landlord.

## Federal rows: the federal pass (Claude Code, 2026-11-20)

Taylor's decision 2026-11-20 (backlog item 13; scope: private rentals and voucher tenancies, not federally assisted or tax-credit properties): nine federal education rows are tagged here and in every jurisdiction, each read from the official text (eCFR, govinfo.gov, the Federal Register; 109 quotations matched): `edu-scra-eviction-protections` (50 U.S.C. §§ 3951, 3931, 3958; the 2026 rent limit of $10,542.60), `edu-bankruptcy-eviction-federal` (11 U.S.C. § 362(a), (b)(22)-(23), (k), (l), (m)), `edu-fair-housing-coverage-federal` (42 U.S.C. §§ 3602(k), 3603(b), 3604(c), 3607(b)(1), 1982), `edu-fair-housing-harassment-federal` (24 C.F.R. §§ 100.600, 100.7; 42 U.S.C. § 3617), `edu-accessible-design-federal` (42 U.S.C. § 3604(f)(3)(C), (f)(4), (f)(7)), `edu-debt-collectors-federal` (15 U.S.C. § 1692a(5)-(6)), `edu-asbestos-demolition-renovation-federal` (40 C.F.R. §§ 61.141, 61.145), `edu-antenna-rights-federal` (47 C.F.R. § 1.4000) and `edu-voucher-repairs-and-rent` (24 C.F.R. §§ 982.404, 982.451, 982.453, 982.507, 982.510). A tenth, `edu-credit-reporting-furnisher-duties` (15 U.S.C. §§ 1681s-2, 1681i(a)(1); 12 C.F.R. §§ 1022.40-1022.43), was added the same day at Taylor's request. This state's existing rows on the same subjects stay, since they carry state detail; no clause changed.

## Model-law comparison: two new topics (Claude Code, 2026-11-21)

Taylor's decision 2026-11-20: compare the library's topic list with the uniform model laws (the 1972 Uniform Residential Landlord and Tenant Act, the Revised URLTA of 2015, and UCC § 3-311) and answer any topic they raise that the library lacked. Two did: checks marked 'paid in full' (`paid-in-full-payments`) and a general duty of good faith (`good-faith-obligation`). This jurisdiction's rows are `edu-paid-in-full-checks-mt` (VERIFIED; Mont. Code Ann. § 30-3-311; Mont. Code Ann. § 30-1-207(2)) and `edu-good-faith-obligation-mt` (VERIFIED; Mont. Code Ann. § 70-24-109). Each was researched from official text by an independent agent; every quotation was machine-checked against the saved page and the quotes are in each row's notes. 

### Declined option, 2026-11-21 (Taylor's decision)

- `edu-no-subordination-clause` tagged here and in every jurisdiction: the library offers no clause subordinating the lease to a mortgage (rule 54 row); the tenant's foreclosure protection is the federal PTFA (`edu-ptfa-foreclosure-tenants`).

## Lead-paint applicability (Claude Code, 2026-11-21)

Uniform federal label correction (rule 62): the shared `lead-based-paint` is now CONDITIONAL/REQUIRED (it was REQUIRED), since the federal disclosure applies only to housing built before 1978; no text changed. The app now adds it automatically, locked, when the property was built before 1978 or its year built isn't entered, and leaves it off for 1978 or later (Taylor's decision 2026-11-21).

## Required-label correction (Claude Code, 2026-11-21)

Taylor's decision 2026-11-21: a clause is REQUIRED only when a statute requires it in the lease (SOP rule 56). Rows labeled REQUIRED whose research found no such statute (basis SERVES_LANDLORD or CONSTRAINED_TERM) are now RECOMMENDED; required text that applies only to some properties is CONDITIONAL/REQUIRED. No clause text changed. This jurisdiction's rows:

- `default-by-tenant` (REQUIRED to RECOMMENDED)
- `assistance-animal-accommodation` (REQUIRED to RECOMMENDED)
- `services-utilities-provided-ks-oh` (REQUIRED to RECOMMENDED)

## Required and Legal duty labels (Claude Code, 2026-11-22)

Taylor's decision 2026-11-22 (SOP 2.19, rule 56): a clause is REQUIRED only when the law requires the text in the lease itself; LEGAL_DUTY when the law requires the landlord to give the tenant the information but not in the lease (the clause is one way to do it); text required only if the landlord uses a term is CONSTRAINED. Each row below was read against its notes' statutory text; the reason is in the row. No clause text changed.

- `foreclosure-disclosure-nv`: RECOMMENDED to CONDITIONAL/LEGAL_DUTY
- `landlord-disclosure-mt`: REQUIRED to LEGAL_DUTY
- `mold-disclosure-mt`: CONDITIONAL to CONDITIONAL/LEGAL_DUTY
- `condominium-unit-lease-contents-mt`: CONDITIONAL to CONDITIONAL/REQUIRED

## Tags for situational clauses (Claude Code, 2026-11-22)

Taylor's decision 2026-11-22: every clause shows a tag. A clause that applies only in some situations now carries the label it has when it applies (SOP rule 56): Recommended where the research found no statute requiring it and it serves the landlord, Optional where it is a term the landlord chooses within the law's limits, Required or Legal duty where its notes' statute was read and requires it.
